=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N rest areas around a lake.
The rest areas are numbered 1, 2, ..., N in clockwise order.
It takes A_i steps to walk clockwise from rest area i to rest area i+1 (where rest area N+1 refers to rest area 1).
The minimum number of steps required to walk clockwise from rest area s to rest area t (s \neq t) is a multiple of M.
Find the number of possible pairs (s,t).

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
A_1 A_2 \dots A_N

Output

Print the answer as an integer.

Constraints


- All input values are integers
- 2 \le N \le 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \le A_i \le 10^9
- 1 \le M \le 10^6

Sample Input 1

4 3
2 1 4 3

Sample Output 1

4


- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 1 to rest area 2 is 2, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 1 to rest area 3 is 3, which is a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 1 to rest area 4 is 7, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 2 to rest area 3 is 1, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 2 to rest area 4 is 5, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 2 to rest area 1 is 8, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 3 to rest area 4 is 4, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 3 to rest area 1 is 7, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 3 to rest area 2 is 9, which is a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 4 to rest area 1 is 3, which is a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 4 to rest area 2 is 5, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 4 to rest area 3 is 6, which is a multiple of 3.

Therefore, there are four possible pairs (s,t).

Sample Input 2

2 1000000
1 1

Sample Output 2

0

Sample Input 3

9 5
9 9 8 2 4 4 3 5 3

Sample Output 3

11

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

import sys
def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]; M = data[1]
    A = data[2:2+N]
    res = [0]*N
    total_mod = 0
    pref = 0
    # Need fill res and total_mod. Could do:
    for i,a in enumerate(A):
        if i < N: res[i] = pref % M  # before adding edge i
        pref += a
    total_mod = pref % M
But careful: For i=N-1, res[N-1] = pref before adding A[N-1], yes. Then pref after adding all A = S. total_mod = pref % M. Good.
    total_counts = [0]*M
    for r in res: total_counts[r]+=1
    prev_counts = [0]*M
    ans=0
    for r in res:
       later = total_counts[r] - prev_counts[r] - 1
       target = (r - total_mod) % M
       earlier = prev_counts[target]
       ans += later + earlier
       prev_counts[r] += 1
    print(ans)
if __name__ == "__main__": main()

```
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    total_mod = 0
    for idx in range(2, 2+N):
        total_mod = (total_mod + data[idx]) % M

    total_counts = [0] * M
    pref = 0
    for idx in range(2, 2+N):
        r = pref % M
        total_counts[r] += 1
        pref += data[idx]

    prev_counts = [0] * M
    pref = 0
    ans = 0
    for idx in range(2, 2+N):
        r = pref % M
        later = total_counts[r] - prev_counts[r] - 1
        target = (r - total_mod) % M
        earlier = prev_counts[target]
        ans += later + earlier
        prev_counts[r] += 1
        pref += data[idx]

    print(ans)
```
Need check second pass: pref starts 0. For idx=2 (A_1), r=P0=0. After processing, pref += A_1. For idx=3, r=P1. Good. After last idx=2+N-1, r=P[N-1]; after processing pref += A_N but no use. Good. total_mod computed as sum all A mod M. Good.

Now, code:
```
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]; M = data[1]
    total_counts = [0] * M
    pref_mod = 0
    for idx in range(2, 2 + N):
        r = pref_mod
        total_counts[r] += 1
        pref_mod = (pref_mod + data[idx]) % M
    total_mod = pref_mod

    prev_counts = [0] * M
    pref_mod = 0
    ans = 0
    for idx in range(2, 2 + N):
        r = pref_mod
        later = total_counts[r] - prev_counts[r] - 1
        target = (r - total_mod) % M
        earlier = prev_counts[target]
        ans += later + earlier
        prev_counts[r] += 1
        pref_mod = (pref_mod + data[idx]) % M

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
Need check first pass: We add data[idx] after counting rest area idx-2. For idx=2+N-1 (last A_N), r = P[N-1] mod M (rest area N), count it, then pref_mod = S mod M. Good. total_mod = S mod M. Second pass similarly: For idx last, r=P[N-1], process, then pref_mod=S mod M but no use. Good.

Let's ensure final code is syntactically correct:
```
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]

    total_counts = [0] * M
    pref_mod = 0
    for i in range(2, 2 + N):
        r = pref_mod
        total_counts[r] += 1
        pref_mod = (pref_mod + data[i]) % M

    total_mod = pref_mod

    prev_counts = [0] * M
    pref_mod = 0
    ans = 0
    for i in range(2, 2 + N):
        r = pref_mod
        later = total_counts[r] - prev_counts[r] - 1
        target = (r - total_mod) % M
        earlier = prev_counts[target]
        ans += later + earlier
        prev_counts[r] += 1
        pref_mod = (pref_mod + data[i]) % M

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
